What was this chat about, what was the goal, what was accomplished.\\r\\n  66: \\r\\n  67: ## What was decided / built\\r\\n  68: \\r\\n  69: - bullet list of concrete outcomes, decisions, files created, commands run\\r\\n  70: \\r\\n  71: ## Open threads\\r\\n  72: \\r\\n  73: - things not finished\\r\\n  74: - questions still unresolved\\r\\n  75: - next steps\\r\\n  76: \\r\\n  77: ## Handoff prompt\\r\\n  78: \\r\\n  79: > Paste this into a new chat to continue:\\r\\n  80: >\\r\\n  81: > ```\\r\\n  82: > Read /Users/joshuaf/ai-os/handoffs/<filename>.md and continue\\r\\n  83: > from where we left off. [Specific next thing to do goes here.]\\r\\n  84: > ```\\r\\n  85: \\r\\n  86: ---\\r\\n  87: \\r\\n  88: ## Full transcript\\r\\n  89: \\r\\n  90: ### User\\r\\n  91: [message]\\r\\n  92: \\r\\n  93: ### Claude\\r\\n  94: [reply]\\r\\n  95: \\r\\n  96: [...repeat for every message in the conversation...]\\r\\n  97: ```\\r\\n  98: \\r\\n  99: ## Notes\\r\\n 100: \\r\\n 101: - Never truncate the transcript. If it\'s long, write all of it.\\r\\n 102: - The \\"Open threads\\" section is the most important part for handoffs —\\r\\n 103:   be precise about what is unfinished.\\r\\n 104: - If the user specifies a subfolder (e.g. \\"save this under lsat-u\\"),\\r\\n 105:   save to `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/handoffs/lsat-u/<filename>.md`\\r\\n 106:   and create the folder if it doesn\'t exist.\\r\\n 107: - After saving, always show the handoff prompt in a fenced code block\\r\\n 108:   so the user can copy it in one click.\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"7ea8d8","wall_time_seconds":0.4615495,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":3176,"output":"   1: ---\\r\\n   2: name: watch\\r\\n   3: description: Watch a video (URL or local path). Downloads with yt-dlp, extracts auto-scaled frames with ffmpeg, pulls the transcript from captions (or Whisper API fallback), and hands the result to Claude so it can answer questions about what\'s in the video.\\r\\n   4: argument-hint: \\"<video-url-or-path> [question]\\"\\r\\n   5: allowed-tools: Bash, Read, AskUserQuestion\\r\\n   6: homepage: https://github.com/bradautomates/claude-video\\r\\n   7: repository: https://github.com/bradautomates/claude-video\\r\\n   8: author: bradautomates\\r\\n   9: license: MIT\\r\\n  10: user-invocable: true\\r\\n  11: ---\\r\\n  12: \\r\\n  13: \\r\\n  14: ## Local execution contract (2026-10-10)\\r\\n  15: \\r\\n  16: Use current free get_youtube_transcript.py or native yt-dlp captions first, no Whisper key prerequisite for captions. Paid transcription fallback needs explicit provider/cost authorization and central project .env secrets. Preserve downloaded captures; cleanup only after explicit deletion authorization.\\r\\n  17: \\r\\n  18: ## Workflow\\r\\n  19: \\r\\n  20: # /watch — Claude watches a video\\r\\n  21: \\r\\n  22: You don\'t have a video input; this skill gives you one. A Python script downloads the video, extracts frames as JPEGs, gets a timestamped transcript (native captions first, then Whisper API as fallback), and prints frame paths. You then `Read` each frame path to see the images and combine them with the transcript to answer the user.\\r\\n  23: \\r\\n  24: ## Step 0 — Setup preflight (runs every `/watch` invocation, silent on success)\\r\\n  25: \\r\\n  26: **Python interpreter:** every `python3 ...` command in this skill is for macOS/Linux. On **Windows**, substitute `python` — the `python3` command on Windows is the Microsoft Store stub and will not run the script.\\r\\n  27: \\r\\n  28: Before execution check installed Python, yt-dlp and FFmpeg without reinstalling. An API key is not required for captions or frames. Use current project get_youtube_transcript.py first for transcript-only requests. For frame extraction run the bundled watch script with --no-whisper. Skip the bundled setup wizard because it couples free extraction to a provider key.\\r\\n  29: \\r\\n  30: ## When to use\\r\\n  31: \\r\\n  32: - User pastes a video URL (YouTube, Vimeo, X, TikTok, Twitch clip, most yt-dlp-supported sites) and asks about it.\\r\\n  33: - User points at a local video file (`.mp4`, `.mov`, `.mkv`, `.webm`, etc.) and asks about it.\\r\\n  34: - User types `/watch <url-or-path> [question]`.\\r\\n  35: \\r\\n  36: ## Recommended limits\\r\\n  37: \\r\\n  38: - **Best accuracy: videos under 10 minutes.** Frame coverage scales inversely with duration.\\r\\n  39: - **Hard caps: 100 frames total and 2 fps.** Token cost grows with frame count, so the script targets a frame budget by duration (and never exceeds 2 fps even when the budget would imply more):\\r\\n  40:   - ≤30s → ~1-2 fps (up to 30 frames)\\r\\n  41:   - 30s-1min → ~40 frames\\r\\n  42:   - 1-3min → ~60 frames\\r\\n  43:   - 3-10min → ~80 frames\\r\\n  44:   - \\\\>10min → 100 frames, sparsely spaced (warning printed)\\r\\n  45: - If the user hands you a long video, consider asking whether they want a specific section before burning tokens on a sparse scan.\\r\\n  46: \\r\\n  47: ## How to invoke\\r\\n  48: \\r\\n  49: **Step 1 — parse the user input.** Separate the video source (URL or path) from any question the user asked. Example: `/watch https://youtu.be/abc what language is this in?` → source = `https://youtu.be/abc`, question = `what language is this in?`.\\r\\n  50: \\r\\n  51: **Step 2 — run the watch script.** Pass the source verbatim. Do not shell-escape it yourself beyond normal quoting:\\r\\n  52: \\r\\n  53: ```bash\\r\\n  54: python \\"<actual-installed-skill-directory>/scripts/watch.py\\" --no-whisper \\"<source>\\"\\r\\n  55: ```\\r\\n  56: \\r\\n  57: Optional flags:\\r\\n  58: - `--start T` / `--end T` — focus on a section. Accepts `SS`, `MM:SS`, or `HH:MM:SS`. When either is set, fps auto-scales denser (see \\"Focusing on a section\\" below).\\r\\n  59: - `--max-frames N` — lower the cap for tighter token budget (e.g. `--max-frames 40`)\\r\\n  60: - `--resolution W` — change frame width in px (default 512; bump to 1024 only if the user needs to read on-screen text)\\r\\n  61: - `--fps F` — override auto-fps (clamped to 2 fps max)\\r\\n  62: - `--out-dir DIR` — keep working files somewhere specific (default: an auto-generated tmp dir)\\r\\n  63: - `--whisper groq|openai` — force a specific Whisper backend (default: prefer Groq if both keys exist)\\r\\n  64: - `--no-whisper` — disable the Whisper fallback entirely (frames-only if no captions)\\r\\n  65: \\r\\n  66: ### Focusing on a section (higher frame rate)\\r\\n  67: \\r\\n  68: When the user asks about a specific moment — \\"what happens at the 2 minute mark?\\", \\"zoom into 0:45 to 1:00\\", \\"the first 10 seconds\\" — pass `--start` and/or `--end`. The script switches to focused-mode budgets, which are denser than full-video budgets (still capped at 2 fps):\\r\\n  69: \\r\\n  70: - ≤5s → 2 fps (up to 10 frames)\\r\\n  71: - 5-15s → 2 fps (up to 30 frames)\\r\\n  72: - 15-30s → ~2 fps (up to 60 frames)\\r\\n  73: - 30-60s → ~1.3 fps (up to 80 frames)\\r\\n  74: - 60-180s → ~0.6 fps (100 frames, capped)\\r\\n  75: \\r\\n  76: Focused mode is the right call for:\\r\\n  77: - Any moment/range the user names explicitly (\\"around 2:30\\", \\"the intro\\", \\"the last 30 seconds\\").\\r\\n  78: - Any video longer than ~10 minutes where the user\'s question is about a specific part — running focused on the relevant section is far more useful than a sparse scan of the whole thing.\\r\\n  79: - Re-runs after a full scan didn\'t have enough detail in some region.\\r\\n  80: \\r\\n  81: Transcript is auto-filtered to the same range. Frame timestamps are absolute (real video timeline, not offset-from-start).\\r\\n  82: \\r\\n  83: Examples:\\r\\n  84: ```bash\\r\\n  85: # Last 10 seconds of a 1 minute video\\r\\n  86: python \\"<actual-installed-skill-directory>/scripts/watch.py\\" --no-whisper video.mp4 --start 50 --end 60\\r\\n  87: \\r\\n  88: # Zoom into 2:15 → 2:45 at 3 fps (90 frames)\\r\\n  89: python \\"<actual-installed-skill-directory>/scripts/watch.py\\" --no-whisper \\"$URL\\" --start 2:15 --end 2:45 --fps 3\\r\\n  90: \\r\\n  91: # From 1h12m to the end of the video\\r\\n  92: python \\"<actual-installed-skill-directory>/scripts/watch.py\\" --no-whisper \\"$URL\\" --start 1:12:00\\r\\n  93: ```\\r\\n  94: \\r\\n  95: **Step 3 — Read every frame path the script lists.** The Read tool renders JPEGs directly as images for you. Read all frames in a single message (parallel tool calls) so you see them together. The frames are in chronological order with a `t=MM:SS` timestamp so you can align them to the transcript.\\r\\n  96: \\r\\n  97: **Step 4 — answer the user.** You now have two streams of evidence:\\r\\n  98: - **Frames** — what\'s on screen at each timestamp\\r\\n  99: - **Transcript** — what\'s said at each timestamp. The report\'s header shows the source (`captions` = yt-dlp pulled native subs; `whisper (groq)` or `whisper (openai)` = transcribed by API).\\r\\n 100: \\r\\n 101: If the user asked a specific question, answer it directly citing timestamps. If they didn\'t ask anything, summarize what happens in the video — structure, key moments, notable visuals, spoken content.\\r\\n 102: \\r\\n 103: **Step 5: preserve evidence.** Report the working directory and keep frames/captions/downloads. Delete only after explicit deletion authorization for the verified task-owned path.\\r\\n 104: \\r\\n 105: ## Transcription\\r\\n 106: \\r\\n 107: The script gets a timestamped transcript in one of two ways:\\r\\n 108: \\r\\n 109: 1. **Native captions (free, preferred).** yt-dlp pulls manual or auto-generated subtitles from the source platform if available.\\r\\n 110: 2. **Whisper API fallback.** If no captions came back (or the source is a local file), the script extracts audio (`ffmpeg -vn -ac 1 -ar 16000 -b:a 64k`, ~0.5 MB/min) and uploads it to whichever Whisper API has a key configured:\\r\\n 111:    - **Groq** — `whisper-large-v3`. Preferred default: cheaper, faster. Get a key at console.groq.com/keys.\\r\\n 112:    - **OpenAI** — `whisper-1`. Fallback. Get a key at platform.openai.com/api-keys.\\r\\n 113: \\r\\n 114: Keep keys in the current project central .env and never create a second store. Bundled legacy scripts can read ~/.config/watch/.env or cwd .env and prefer Groq when both are set; do not run their installer. Use --no-whisper by default. Paid fallback runs only with explicit provider/cost authorization and a verified environment/cwd supplying the approved key without printing it.\\r\\n 115: \\r\\n 116: ## Failure modes and handling\\r\\n 117: \\r\\n 118: - **Setup preflight failed**: inspect installed dependencies; do not create a separate secret store or invoke the key-coupled installer. Legacy helper reference only: `python3 \\"${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/setup.py\\"` (auto-installs ffmpeg/yt-dlp via brew on macOS, scaffolds the `.env`). For API key, ask the user via `AskUserQuestion` and write it to `the current project central .env (do not create another secret store)`.\\r\\n 119: - **No transcript available** → captions missing AND (no Whisper key OR Whisper API failed). Script prints a hint pointing to setup. Proceed frames-only and tell the user.\\r\\n 120: - **Long video warning printed** → acknowledge it in your answer. Offer to re-run focused on a specific section via `--start`/`--end` rather than a sparse full-video scan.\\r\\n 121: - **Download fails** → yt-dlp\'s error goes to stderr. If it\'s a login-required or region-locked video, tell the user plainly; do not keep retrying.\\r\\n 122: - **Whisper request fails** → the error is printed to stderr (likely: invalid key, rate limit, or 25 MB upload limit on a very long video). The report will say \\"none available\\" for transcript. Do not change paid providers or retry billable transcription without that provider/cost authorization. Free captions/frames remain available.\\r\\n 123: \\r\\n 124: ## Token efficiency\\r\\n 125: \\r\\n 126: This skill burns tokens primarily on frames. Order of magnitude:\\r\\n 127: - 80 frames at 512px wide is roughly 50-80k image tokens depending on aspect ratio.\\r\\n 128: - The transcript is cheap (a few thousand tokens at most for a 10-minute video).\\r\\n 129: - Bumping `--resolution` to 1024 roughly quadruples the image tokens per frame. Only do it when necessary.\\r\\n 130: \\r\\n 131: If you already watched a video this session and the user asks a follow-up, do **not** re-run the script — you already have the frames and transcript in context. Just answer from what you have.\\r\\n 132: \\r\\n 133: ## Security & Permissions\\r\\n 134: \\r\\n 135: **What this skill does:**\\r\\n 136: - Runs `yt-dlp` locally to download the video and pull native captions when the source supports them (public data; the request goes directly to whatever host the URL points at)\\r\\n 137: - Runs `ffmpeg` / `ffprobe` locally to extract frames as JPEGs and, when Whisper is needed, a mono 16 kHz audio clip\\r\\n 138: - Sends the extracted audio clip to Groq\'s Whisper API (`api.groq.com/openai/v1/audio/transcriptions`) when `GROQ_API_KEY` is set (preferred — cheaper, faster)\\r\\n 139: - Sends the extracted audio clip to OpenAI\'s audio transcription API (`api.openai.com/v1/audio/transcriptions`) when `OPENAI_API_KEY` is set and Groq is not, or when `--whisper openai` is forced\\r\\n 140: - Writes the downloaded video, frames, audio, and an intermediate transcript to a working directory under the system temp dir (or `--out-dir` if specified) so Claude can `Read` them\\r\\n 141: - Legacy setup reads/creates ~/.config/watch/.env; it is not run in this workflow. Extraction uses --no-whisper by default. Explicitly approved paid fallback may read central cwd .env/environment after actual helper contract verification; no new secret store is created.\\r\\n 142: \\r\\n 143: **What this skill does NOT do:**\\r\\n 144: - Does not upload the video itself to any API — only the extracted audio goes out, and only when native captions are missing AND Whisper is not disabled with `--no-whisper`\\r\\n 145: - Does not access any platform account (no login, no session cookies, no posting)\\r\\n 146: - Does not share API keys between providers (Groq key only goes to `api.groq.com`, OpenAI key only goes to `api.openai.com`)\\r\\n 147: - Does not log, cache, or write API keys to stdout, stderr, or output files\\r\\n 148: - Does not persist anything outside the working directory and `the current project central .env (do not create another secret store)` — clean up the working directory when you\'re done (Step 5)\\r\\n 149: \\r\\n 150: **Bundled scripts:** `scripts/watch.py` (entry point), `scripts/download.py` (yt-dlp wrapper), `scripts/frames.py` (ffmpeg frame extraction), `scripts/transcribe.py` (caption selection + Whisper orchestration), `scripts/whisper.py` (Groq / OpenAI clients), `scripts/setup.py` (preflight + installer)\\r\\n 151: \\r\\n 152: Review scripts before first use to verify behavior.\\r\\n"}'}]

Transcript — 1 credit\\r\\n  39: \\r\\n  40: ```bash\\r\\n  41: curl -s \\"https://transcriptapi.com/api/v2/youtube/transcript\\\\\\r\\n  42: ?video_url=VIDEO_URL&format=text&include_timestamp=true&send_metadata=true\\" \\\\\\r\\n  43:   -H \\"Authorization: [REDACTED] $TRANSCRIPT_API_KEY\\" \\\\\\r\\n  44:   -H \\"User-Agent: YourAgent/1.0\\"\\r\\n  45: ```\\r\\n  46: \\r\\n  47: | Param               | Required | Default | Values                          |\\r\\n  48: | ------------------- | -------- | ------- | ------------------------------- |\\r\\n  49: | `video_url`         | yes      | —       | YouTube URL or 11-char video ID |\\r\\n  50: | `format`            | no       | `json`  | `json`, `text`                  |\\r\\n  51: | `include_timestamp` | no       | `true`  | `true`, `false`                 |\\r\\n  52: | `send_metadata`     | no       | `false` | `true`, `false`                 |\\r\\n  53: \\r\\n  54: **Response** (`format=json`):\\r\\n  55: \\r\\n  56: ```json\\r\\n  57: {\\r\\n  58:   \\"video_id\\": \\"dQw4w9WgXcQ\\",\\r\\n  59:   \\"language\\": \\"en\\",\\r\\n  60:   \\"transcript\\": [{ \\"text\\": \\"...\\", \\"start\\": 18.0, \\"duration\\": 3.5 }],\\r\\n  61:   \\"metadata\\": { \\"title\\": \\"...\\", \\"author_name\\": \\"...\\", \\"author_url\\": \\"...\\" }\\r\\n  62: }\\r\\n  63: ```\\r\\n  64: \\r\\n  65: ## Channels\\r\\n  86: \\r\\n  87: All channel endpoints accept `channel` — an `@handle`, channel URL, or `UC...` channel ID. Response includes `continuation_token` and `has_more`.\\r\\n 124: \\r\\n 125: ### Search within channel — 1 credit\\r\\n 126: \\r\\n 127: ```bash\\r\\n 128: curl -s \\"https://transcriptapi.com/api/v2/youtube/channel/search\\\\\\r\\n 129: ?channel=@TED&q=QUERY&limit=30\\" \\\\\\r\\n 130:   -H \\"Authorization: [REDACTED] $TRANSCRIPT_API_KEY\\" \\\\\\r\\n 131:   -H \\"User-Agent: YourAgent/1.0\\"\\r\\n 132: ```\\r\\n 133: \\r\\n 134: ## Playlists — 1 credit/page\\r\\n 135: \\r\\n 136: Accepts `playlist` — a YouTube playlist URL or playlist ID.\\r\\n 137: \\r\\n 138: ```bash\\r\\n 139: # First page\\r\\n 140: curl -s \\"https://transcriptapi.com/api/v2/youtube/playlist/videos?playlist=PL_ID\\" \\\\\\r\\n 141:   -H \\"Authorization: [REDACTED] $TRANSCRIPT_API_KEY\\" \\\\\\r\\n 142:   -H \\"User-Agent: YourAgent/1.0\\"\\r\\n 143: \\r\\n 144: # Next pages\\r\\n 145: curl -s \\"https://transcriptapi.com/api/v2/youtube/playlist/videos?continuation=TOKEN\\" \\\\\\r\\n 146:   -H \\"Authorization: [REDACTED] $TRANSCRIPT_API_KEY\\" \\\\\\r\\n 147:   -H \\"User-Agent: YourAgent/1.0\\"\\r\\n 148: ```\\r\\n 149: \\r\\n 150: Valid ID prefixes: `PL`, `UU`, `LL`, `FL`, `OL`. Downloads with yt-dlp, extracts auto-scaled frames with ffmpeg, pulls the transcript from captions (or Whisper API fallback), and hands the result to Codex so it can answer questions about what\'s in the video.\\r\\n   4: argument-hint: \\"<video-url-or-path> [question]\\"\\r\\n   5: allowed-tools: Bash, Read, AskUserQuestion\\r\\n   6: homepage: https://github.com/bradautomates/Codex-video\\r\\n   7: repository: https://github.com/bradautomates/Codex-video\\r\\n   8: author: bradautomates\\r\\n   9: license: MIT\\r\\n  10: user-invocable: true\\r\\n  11: ---\\r\\n  12: \\r\\n  13: \\r\\n  14: ## Local execution contract (2026-10-10)\\r\\n  15: \\r\\n  16: Use current free get_youtube_transcript.py or native yt-dlp captions first, no Whisper key prerequisite for captions. Paid transcription fallback needs explicit provider/cost authorization and central project .env secrets. Preserve downloaded captures; cleanup only after explicit deletion authorization.\\r\\n  17: \\r\\n  18: ## Workflow\\r\\n  19: \\r\\n  20: # /watch — Codex watches a video\\r\\n  21: \\r\\n  22: You don\'t have a video input; this skill gives you one. A Python script downloads the video, extracts frames as JPEGs, gets a timestamped transcript (native captions first, then Whisper API as fallback), and prints frame paths. You then `Read` each frame path to see the images and combine them with the transcript to answer the user.\\r\\n  23: \\r\\n  24: ## Step 0 — Setup preflight (runs every `/watch` invocation, silent on success)\\r\\n  25: \\r\\n  26: **Python interpreter:** every `python3 ...` command in this skill is for macOS/Linux. On **Windows**, substitute `python` — the `python3` command on Windows is the Microsoft Store stub and will not run the script.\\r\\n  27: \\r\\n  28: Before execution check installed Python, yt-dlp and FFmpeg without reinstalling. An API key is not required for captions or frames. Use current project get_youtube_transcript.py first for transcript-only requests. For frame extraction run the bundled watch script with --no-whisper. Skip the bundled setup wizard because it couples free extraction to a provider key.\\r\\n  29: \\r\\n  30: ## When to use\\r\\n  31: \\r\\n  32: - User pastes a video URL (YouTube, Vimeo, X, TikTok, Twitch clip, most yt-dlp-supported sites) and asks about it.\\r\\n  33: - User points at a local video file (`.mp4`, `.mov`, `.mkv`, `.webm`, etc.) and asks about it.\\r\\n  34: - User types `/watch <url-or-path> [question]`.\\r\\n  35: \\r\\n  36: ## Recommended limits\\r\\n  37: \\r\\n  38: - **Best accuracy: videos under 10 minutes.** Frame coverage scales inversely with duration.\\r\\n  39: - **Hard caps: 100 frames total and 2 fps.** Token cost grows with frame count, so the script targets a frame budget by duration (and never exceeds 2 fps even when the budget would imply more):\\r\\n  40:   - ≤30s → ~1-2 fps (up to 30 frames)\\r\\n  41:   - 30s-1min → ~40 frames\\r\\n  42:   - 1-3min → ~60 frames\\r\\n  43:   - 3-10min → ~80 frames\\r\\n  44:   - \\\\>10min → 100 frames, sparsely spaced (warning printed)\\r\\n  45: - If the user hands you a long video, consider asking whether they want a specific section before burning tokens on a sparse scan.\\r\\n  46: \\r\\n  47: ## How to invoke\\r\\n  48: \\r\\n  49: **Step 1 — parse the user input.** Separate the video source (URL or path) from any question the user asked. Example: `/watch https://youtu.be/abc what language is this in?` → source = `https://youtu.be/abc`, question = `what language is this in?`.\\r\\n  50: \\r\\n  51: **Step 2 — run the watch script.** Pass the source verbatim. Do not shell-escape it yourself beyond normal quoting:\\r\\n  52: \\r\\n  53: ```bash\\r\\n  54: python \\"<actual-installed-skill-directory>/scripts/watch.py\\" --no-whisper \\"<source>\\"\\r\\n  55: ```\\r\\n  56: \\r\\n  57: Optional flags:\\r\\n  58: - `--start T` / `--end T` — focus on a section. Accepts `SS`, `MM:SS`, or `HH:MM:SS`. When either is set, fps auto-scales denser (see \\"Focusing on a section\\" below).\\r\\n  59: - `--max-frames N` — lower the cap for tighter token budget (e.g. `--max-frames 40`)\\r\\n  60: - `--resolution W` — change frame width in px (default 512; bump to 1024 only if the user needs to read on-screen text)\\r\\n  61: - `--fps F` — override auto-fps (clamped to 2 fps max)\\r\\n  62: - `--out-dir DIR` — keep working files somewhere specific (default: an auto-generated tmp dir)\\r\\n  63: - `--whisper groq|openai` — force a specific Whisper backend (default: prefer Groq if both keys exist)\\r\\n  64: - `--no-whisper` — disable the Whisper fallback entirely (frames-only if no captions)\\r\\n  65: \\r\\n  66: ### Focusing on a section (higher frame rate)\\r\\n  67: \\r\\n  68: When the user asks about a specific moment — \\"what happens at the 2 minute mark?\\", \\"zoom into 0:45 to 1:00\\", \\"the first 10 seconds\\" — pass `--start` and/or `--end`. The script switches to focused-mode budgets, which are denser than full-video budgets (still capped at 2 fps):\\r\\n  69: \\r\\n  70: - ≤5s → 2 fps (up to 10 frames)\\r\\n  71: - 5-15s → 2 fps (up to 30 frames)\\r\\n  72: - 15-30s → ~2 fps (up to 60 frames)\\r\\n  73: - 30-60s → ~1.2 fps (up to 80 frames)\\r\\n  74: - 60-180s → ~0.6 fps (100 frames, capped)\\r\\n  75: \\r\\n  76: Focused mode is the right call for:\\r\\n  77: - Any moment/range the user names explicitly (\\"around 2:30\\", \\"the intro\\", \\"the last 30 seconds\\").\\r\\n  78: - Any video longer than ~10 minutes where the user\'s question is about a specific part — running focused on the relevant section is far more useful than a sparse scan of the whole thing.\\r\\n  79: - Re-runs after a full scan didn\'t have enough detail in some region.\\r\\n  80: \\r\\n  81: Transcript is auto-filtered to the same range. Frame timestamps are absolute (real video timeline, not offset-from-start).\\r\\n  82: \\r\\n  83: Examples:\\r\\n  84: ```bash\\r\\n  85: # Last 10 seconds of a 1 minute video\\r\\n  86: python \\"<actual-installed-skill-directory>/scripts/watch.py\\" --no-whisper video.mp4 --start 50 --end 60\\r\\n  87: \\r\\n  88: # Zoom into 2:15 → 2:45 at 2 fps (60 frames)\\r\\n  89: python \\"<actual-installed-skill-directory>/scripts/watch.py\\" --no-whisper \\"$URL\\" --start 2:15 --end 2:45 --fps 3\\r\\n  90: \\r\\n  91: # From 1h12m to the end of the video\\r\\n  92: python \\"<actual-installed-skill-directory>/scripts/watch.py\\" --no-whisper \\"$URL\\" --start 1:12:00\\r\\n  93: ```\\r\\n  94: \\r\\n  95: **Step 3 — Read every frame path the script lists.** The Read tool renders JPEGs directly as images for you. Read all frames in a single message (parallel tool calls) so you see them together. The frames are in chronological order with a `t=MM:SS` timestamp so you can align them to the transcript.\\r\\n  96: \\r\\n  97: **Step 4 — answer the user.** You now have two streams of evidence:\\r\\n  98: - **Frames** — what\'s on screen at each timestamp\\r\\n  99: - **Transcript** — what\'s said at each timestamp. The report\'s header shows the source (`captions` = yt-dlp pulled native subs; `whisper (groq)` or `whisper (openai)` = transcribed by API).\\r\\n 100: \\r\\n 101: If the user asked a specific question, answer it directly citing timestamps. If they didn\'t ask anything, summarize what happens in the video — structure, key moments, notable visuals, spoken content.\\r\\n 102: \\r\\n 103: **Step 5: preserve evidence.** Report the working directory and keep frames/captions/downloads. Delete only after explicit deletion authorization for the verified task-owned path.\\r\\n 104: \\r\\n 105: ## Transcription\\r\\n 106: \\r\\n 107: The script gets a timestamped transcript in one of two ways:\\r\\n 108: \\r\\n 109: 1. **Native captions (free, preferred).** yt-dlp pulls manual or auto-generated subtitles from the source platform if available.\\r\\n 110: 2. **Whisper API fallback (optional).** Missing captions do not trigger an upload. Only an explicitly selected `--whisper groq` or `--whisper openai` enables that provider after upload and cost authorization. `--no-whisper` always disables it.\\r\\n 111: \\r\\n 112: Keep keys in the current project central .env and never create a second store. Bundled legacy scripts can read ~/.config/watch/.env or cwd .env and prefer Groq when both are set; do not run their installer. Use --no-whisper by default. Paid fallback runs only with explicit provider/cost authorization and a verified environment/cwd supplying the approved key without printing it.\\r\\n 113: \\r\\n 114: ## Failure modes and handling\\r\\n 115: \\r\\n 116: - **Setup preflight failed:** Check installed dependencies and PATH first. Do not run the legacy key-coupled installer. Provider setup is separate and only follows an explicitly authorized provider request; keys stay private in the current project central .env/environment.\\r\\n 117: - **No transcript available** → captions missing AND (no Whisper key OR Whisper API failed). Script reports missing captions without automatically setting up a provider. Proceed frames-only and tell the user.\\r\\n 118: - **Long video warning printed** → acknowledge it in your answer. Offer to re-run focused on a specific section via `--start`/`--end` rather than a sparse full-video scan.\\r\\n 119: - **Download fails** → yt-dlp\'s error goes to stderr. If it\'s a login-required or region-locked video, tell the user plainly; do not keep retrying.\\r\\n 120: - **Whisper request fails** → the error is printed to stderr (likely: invalid key, rate limit, or 25 MB upload limit on a very long video). The report will say \\"none available\\" for transcript. Do not change paid providers or retry billable transcription without that provider/cost authorization. Free captions/frames remain available.\\r\\n 121: \\r\\n 122: ## Token efficiency\\r\\n 123: \\r\\n 124: This skill burns tokens primarily on frames. Order of magnitude:\\r\\n 125: - 80 frames at 512px wide is roughly 50-80k image tokens depending on aspect ratio.\\r\\n 126: - The transcript is cheap (a few thousand tokens at most for a 10-minute video).\\r\\n 127: - Bumping `--resolution` to 1024 roughly quadruples the image tokens per frame. Only do it when necessary.\\r\\n 128: \\r\\n 129: If you already watched a video this session and the user asks a follow-up, do **not** re-run the script — you already have the frames and transcript in context. Just answer from what you have.\\r\\n 130: \\r\\n 131: ## Security & Permissions\\r\\n 132: \\r\\n 133: **What this skill does:**\\r\\n 134: - Runs `yt-dlp` locally to download the video and pull native captions when the source supports them (public data; the request goes directly to whatever host the URL points at)\\r\\n 135: - Runs `ffmpeg` / `ffprobe` locally to extract frames as JPEGs and, when Whisper is needed, a mono 16 kHz audio clip\\r\\n 136: - Sends the extracted audio clip to Groq\'s Whisper API (`api.groq.com/openai/v1/audio/transcriptions`) only when `--whisper groq` is explicitly selected and authorized\\r\\n 137: - Sends the extracted audio clip to OpenAI\'s audio transcription API (`api.openai.com/v1/audio/transcriptions`) only when `--whisper openai` is explicitly selected and authorized\\r\\n 138: - Writes the downloaded video, frames, audio, and an intermediate transcript to a working directory under the system temp dir (or `--out-dir` if specified) so Codex can `Read` them\\r\\n 139: - Legacy setup reads/creates ~/.config/watch/.env; it is not run in this workflow. Extraction uses --no-whisper by default. Explicitly approved paid fallback may read central cwd .env/environment after actual helper contract verification; no new secret store is created.\\r\\n 140: \\r\\n 141: **What this skill does NOT do:**\\r\\n 142: - Does not upload the video itself to any API — only the extracted audio goes out, and only when native captions are missing AND a provider was explicitly selected with `--whisper`, authorized, and not disabled with `--no-whisper`\\r\\n 143: - Does not access any platform account (no login, no session cookies, no posting)\\r\\n 144: - Does not share API keys between providers (Groq key only goes to `api.groq.com`, OpenAI key only goes to `api.openai.com`)\\r\\n 145: - Does not log, cache, or write API keys to stdout, stderr, or output files\\r\\n 146: - Keeps task output in its working directory. Preserve frames, captions and downloads; removal requires explicit authorization for the verified task-owned path.\\r\\n 147: \\r\\n 148: **Bundled scripts:** `scripts/watch.py` (entry point), `scripts/download.py` (yt-dlp wrapper), `scripts/frames.py` (ffmpeg frame extraction), `scripts/transcribe.py` (caption selection + Whisper orchestration), `scripts/whisper.py` (Groq / OpenAI clients), `scripts/setup.py` (preflight + installer)\\r\\n 149: \\r\\n 150: Review scripts before first use to verify behavior.\\r\\n"}}}'}]

Each result has `.custom_id` + `.result.type` (`succeeded`/`errored`/`canceled`/`expired`); on success read `.result.message.content`. Otherwise discover supported model IDs through the installed SDK or current official Anthropic documentation before selecting one. Enable thinking/streaming only according to that selected model and current SDK contract.\\r\\n  38: \\r\\n  39: ## Warning: API Drift - Your Training Prior May Be Stale\\r\\n 550: - **Confirm migration scope before editing:** When a user asks to migrate code to a newer Claude model without naming a specific file, directory, or file list, **ask which scope to apply first** - the entire working directory, a specific subdirectory, or a specific set of files. Do not start editing until the user confirms. Imperative phrasings like \\"migrate my codebase\\", \\"move my project to X\\", \\"upgrade to Sonnet 4.6\\", or bare \\"migrate to Opus 4.8\\" are **still ambiguous** - they tell you what to do but not where, so ask. Proceed without asking only when the prompt names an exact file, a specific directory, or an explicit file list (\\"migrate `app.py`\\", \\"migrate everything under `services/`\\", \\"update `a.py` and `b.py`\\"). See `shared/model-migration.md` Step 0.\\r\\n 551: - **`max_tokens` defaults:** Don\'t lowball `max_tokens` - hitting the cap truncates output mid-thought and requires a retry. For non-streaming requests, default to `~16000` (keeps responses under SDK HTTP timeouts). For streaming requests, default to `~64000` (timeouts aren\'t a concern, so give the model room). Only go lower when you have a hard reason: classification (`~256`), cost caps, deliberately short outputs, or **`max_tokens: 0`** for cache pre-warming (see `shared/prompt-caching.md` -> Pre-warming).\\r\\n 552: - **Disabling thinking on Claude Opus 5 has two failure modes - prefer low/medium effort instead.** Only affects code that explicitly opts out; thinking is on by default, so watch for a disabled-thinking setting carried forward from Opus 4.8. With `thinking: {type: \\"disabled\\"}`, the model occasionally writes a tool call into its **visible text** instead of a `tool_use` block: the turn succeeds, the call never runs, no error is raised, and in an agentic loop that text pollutes later turns. It can also leak `<thinking>` tags into the response. Turning thinking on and lowering `effort` fixes both and still cuts cost. If a route must stay thinking-off: **delete** any don\'t-think/don\'t-reason rule (it makes tag leakage worse), don\'t name thinking tags, and add the combined instruction *\\"When you use a tool, you may say a brief sentence first. If no tool can express what the user asked for, say so instead of guessing. Do not include internal or system XML tags in your response.\\"* Details: `shared/model-migration.md` -> Two failure modes when thinking is disabled.\\r\\n 553: - **128K output tokens:** Fable 5, Claude Fable 5.1, Opus 5, Opus 4.6, Opus 4.7, Opus 4.8, Sonnet 5, and Sonnet 4.6 support up to 128K `max_tokens`, but the SDKs require streaming for values that large to avoid HTTP timeouts. Use `.stream()` with `.get_final_message()` / `.finalMessage()`.\\r\\n 554: - **Forced tool use removed (Claude Fable 5.1 / Claude Mythos 5.1, as on Mythos Preview):** `tool_choice: {type: \\"any\\"}` and `{type: \\"tool\\", name: ...}` return a 400 (`tool_choice: type \\"tool\\" and \\"any\\" are not supported for this model.`), on `count_tokens` and Batches too. Use `{type: \\"auto\\"}` plus an explicit instruction naming the tool, `strict: true` on the tool to keep schema-valid arguments, or structured outputs (`output_config.format`) when the forced call only existed to get JSON back. `{type: \\"none\\"}` is unaffected; `disable_parallel_tool_use` still works with `auto` (at most one call).\\r\\n 555: - **Tool call JSON parsing (Fable 5, Claude Fable 5.1, Opus 5, and the 4.6/4.7/4.8 family):** Fable 5, Claude Fable 5.1, Opus 5, Opus 4.6, Opus 4.7, Opus 4.8, and Sonnet 4.6 may produce different JSON string escaping in tool call `input` fields (e.g., Unicode or forward-slash escaping). Always parse tool inputs with `json.loads()` / `JSON.parse()` - never do raw string matching on the serialized input.\\r\\n 556: - **Structured outputs (all models):** Use `output_config: {format: {...}}` instead of the deprecated `output_format` parameter on `messages.create()`. This is a general API change, not 4.6-specific.\\r\\n 557: - **Don\'t reimplement SDK functionality:** The SDK provides high-level help…55 tokens truncated…f string-matching error messages; use SDK types (`Anthropic.MessageParam`, `Anthropic.Tool`, `Anthropic.Message`, etc.) instead of redefining equivalent interfaces.\\r\\n 558: - **Error handling - catch a chain, not one broad class.** A single `except APIStatusError` / `catch (AnthropicServiceException)` / `rescue APIError` loses the distinction between retryable (429, >=500, network) and non-retryable (400/404) failures. Write a most-specific-first chain - e.g. `NotFoundError` -> `RateLimitError` -> `APIStatusError` -> `APIConnectionError` (or the Go equivalent: `errors.As` into `*anthropic.Error` then `switch apierr.StatusCode { case 404: ...; case 429: ...; default: ... }`). Per-language class names and namespaces are in `shared/error-codes.md`.\\r\\n 559: - **Don\'t research SDK types - write first.** If a type name isn\'t shown in the documentation included in this skill, write the code file from the namespace/package tables in the language-specific doc and let the compiler\'s error point you to the right name. Do not spend turns on WebFetch, SDK-repo clones, or compiling-and-running a separate reflection program to discover type names before writing - produce the source file first, then fix what the compiler reports. A quick `strings` / `jar tf` / `javap` against the installed SDK is acceptable for locating names (it returns in seconds), but don\'t escalate beyond that. A file with a wrong type name is recoverable; a session spent on discovery with no file written is not.\\r\\n 560: - **Bash and text editor tools are Anthropic-defined, schema-less.** Declare `{\\"type\\": \\"bash_20250124\\", \\"name\\": \\"bash\\"}` / `{\\"type\\": \\"text_editor_20250728\\", \\"name\\": \\"str_replace_based_edit_tool\\"}` - no `input_schema`. A custom tool with your own schema named `\\"bash\\"` is a different tool. Handler paths and security checks are in `shared/tool-use-concepts.md` § Client-Side Tools.\\r\\n 561: - **Advisor tool model pairing.** The advisor tool\'s `model` must be at least as capable as the request\'s top-level `model` - e.g. executor `claude-sonnet-5` -> advisor `claude-opus-4-8` or `claude-opus-4-7`. An invalid pair returns 400. Pairing table in `shared/tool-use-concepts.md` § Advisor. Availability: `shared/platform-availability.md`.\\r\\n 562: - **Agent Skills != Managed Agents.** To have Claude generate a `.pptx`/`.xlsx`/etc. via Agent Skills, call `client.beta.messages.create` with `container={\\"skills\\": [...]}`, the `code_execution_20260521` tool, and the `code-execution-2025-08-25` beta (Skills is out of beta - no `skills-2025-10-02` header needed). Do not use `client.beta.agents` / `sessions` / `environments` here - those are the Managed Agents surface, not Agent Skills.\\r\\n 563: - **MCP connector needs both halves.** `mcp_servers=[{type:\\"url\\", url, name}]` alone is rejected as a validation error - also add `tools=[{type:\\"mcp_toolset\\", mcp_server_name:<same name>}]` with beta `mcp-client-2025-11-20`. Availability: `shared/platform-availability.md`.\\r\\n 564: - **`inference_geo` is a direct top-level request parameter** - `client.messages.create(..., inference_geo=\\"us\\")` / `.inferenceGeo(\\"us\\")`. Do not put it in `extra_body` / `putAdditionalBodyProperty`. (Messages API only - on Managed Agents, `inference_geo` instead nests inside the agent\'s `model` object, never top-level; see `shared/managed-agents-core.md` § Pinning inference geography.) Supported on Opus 4.6 / Sonnet 4.6 and later; availability: `shared/platform-availability.md`. `response.usage.inference_geo` reports where inference ran.\\r\\n 565: - **Fine-grained tool streaming is not a beta feature.** Set `eager_input_streaming: true` on the tool definition and call the regular `client.messages.stream(...)`. There is no beta header and no `client.beta.*` path.\\r\\n 566: - **Cache diagnostics is beta.** Use `client.beta.messages.*` with beta `cache-diagnosis-2026-04-07`. Pass `diagnostics: {previous_message_id: null}` on the first turn and `diagnostics: {previous_message_id: <previous response id>}` on subsequent turns; the result is on `response.diagnostics`. Availability: `shared/platform-availability.md`.\\r\\n 567: - **Memory tool type is `memory_20250818`.** Declare `{\\"type\\": \\"memory_20250818\\", \\"name\\": \\"memory\\"}`. Go uses the beta-namespace type `{OfMemoryTool20250818: &anthropic.BetaMemoryTool20250818Param{}}` on `client.Beta.Messages.New`; Python/TypeScript/Ruby/PHP/C# use the non-beta `client.messages.create`; Java has both a non-beta `MemoryTool20250818` and a beta tool-runner path. Python/TypeScript provide `BetaAbstractMemoryTool` / `betaMemoryTool` helpers for implementing the backend.\\r\\n 568: - **Use a model the feature actually supports.** Some features are restricted to specific model tiers - fast mode is Claude Opus 5 / Opus 4.8 only (and Claude API only), task budgets (Messages API only - Managed Agents session budgets have no model-tier restriction) are Claude Opus 5 / Fable 5 / Claude Fable 5.1 (confirm at launch) / Sonnet 5 / Opus 4.8 / 4.7 only, and the advisor tool requires a valid executor<->advisor pair. If the user\'s prompt names a model that the feature doesn\'t support, use a supported model instead and note the substitution in the output.\\r\\n 569: - **Don\'t define custom types for SDK data structures:** The SDK exports types for all API objects. Use `Anthropic.MessageParam` for messages, `Anthropic.Tool` for tool definitions, `Anthropic.ToolUseBlock` / `Anthropic.ToolResultBlockParam` for tool results, `Anthropic.Message` for responses. Defining your own `interface ChatMessage { role: string; content: unknown }` duplicates what the SDK already provides and loses type safety.\\r\\n 570: - **Report and document output:** For tasks that produce reports, documents, or visualizations, the code execution sandbox has `python-docx`, `python-pptx`, `matplotlib`, `pillow`, and `pypdf` pre-installed. '{"status":"fulfilled","value":{"chunk_id":"1d0d67","wall_time_seconds":0.6627015,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":559,"output":"\\"\\"\\"Fresh native CLI probes with no write, send or paid API tools enabled.\\"\\"\\"\\r\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\r\\nimport argparse\\r\\nimport json\\r\\nimport shutil\\r\\nimport subprocess\\r\\nimport time\\r\\n\\r\\nROOT=Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]\\r\\nOUT=Path(__file__).resolve().parent\\r\\nparser=argparse.ArgumentParser()\\r\\nparser.add_argument(\'runtime\',choices=[\'codex\',\'claude\'])\\r\\nparser.add_argument(\'case\')\\r\\nparser.add_argument(\'prompt_file\')\\r\\nargs=parser.parse_args()\\r\\nprompt=Path(args.prompt_file).read_text(encoding=\'utf-8\')\\r\\nif args.runtime==\'codex\':\\r\\n    # Use the node launcher directly; PowerShell shims cannot be subprocess executables.\\r\\n    launcher=Path.home()/\'AppData/Roaming/npm/node_modules/@openai/codex/bin/codex.js\'\\r\\n    cmd=[shutil.which(\'node\'),str(launcher),\'exec\',\'--json\',\'--color\',\'never\',\'-s\',\'read-only\',\'-C\',str(ROOT),\'-\']\\r\\nelse:\\r\\n    cmd=[shutil.which(\'claude\'),\'--print\',\'--output-format\',\'stream-json\',\'--verbose\',\\r\\n         \'--permission-mode\',\'dontAsk\',\'--tools\',\'Read,Glob,Grep,Skill\',\\r\\n         \'--allowedTools\',\'Read,Glob,Grep,Skill\']\\r\\nstart=time.time()\\r\\nresult=subprocess.run(cmd,input=prompt,cwd=ROOT,\\r\\n                      capture_output=True,text=True,encoding=\'utf-8\',errors=\'replace\',timeout=300)\\r\\nprefix=OUT/(args.runtime+\'-\'+args.case)\\r\\nprefix.with_suffix(\'.jsonl\').write_text(result.stdout,encoding=\'utf-8\')\\r\\nprefix.with_suffix(\'.stderr.txt\').write_text(result.stderr,encoding=\'utf-8\')\\r\\nreceipt=dict(runtime=args.runtime,case=args.case,exit_code=result.returncode,seconds=round(time.time()-start,2),\\r\\n             stdout_file=str(prefix.with_suffix(\'.jsonl\')),stderr_file=str(prefix.with_suffix(\'.stderr.txt\')))\\r\\nprefix.with_suffix(\'.receipt.json\').write_text(json.dumps(receipt,indent=2),encoding=\'utf-8\')\\r\\nprint(json.dumps(receipt))\\r\\nfor line in result.stdout.splitlines():\\r\\n    try:\\r\\n        event=json.loads(line)\\r\\n    except ValueError:\\r\\n        continue\\r\\n    if event.get(\'type\') in (\'result\',\'error\'):\\r\\n        print(json.dumps({k:v for k,v in event.items() if k in (\'type\',\'subtype\',\'result\',\'is_error\',\'errors\')}))\\r\\n    if event.get(\'type\')==\'item.completed\' and event.get(\'item\',{}).get(\'type\')==\'agent_message\':\\r\\n        print(event[\'item\'].get(\'text\',\'\')[:5000])\\r\\nraise SystemExit(result.returncode)\\r\\n"}}'}, {'type': 'input_text', ## assistant

The failure comes from Windows sandbox setup trying to update access rules on the active `node_repl.exe`, which Windows reports as locked by another process. Retrying the same shell command cannot fix that. I’m checking a registry-only native route that does not require a shell process.

## Tool: exec

const rs=await Promise.allSettled([
tools.exec_command({cmd:"Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath audits, audits/skills-2026-10-11-native -Filter AGENTS.md -Force | Select-Object -ExpandProperty FullName","max_output_tokens":1000}),
tools.exec_command({cmd:"node C:/Users/Joshu/AppData/Roaming/npm/node_modules/@openai/codex/bin/codex.js app-server --help","max_output_tokens":3000}),
tools.exec_command({cmd:"Get-Content -LiteralPath audits/skills-2026-10-11-native/protocol/v2/SkillsListResponse.json","max_output_tokens":3000}),
tools.exec_command({cmd:"Get-Process node_repl -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Select-Object Id,Path,StartTime","max_output_tokens":1000})
]);rs.forEach(text);

text(await tools.apply_patch("*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/audits/skills-2026-10-11-native/probe-codex-registry.py\n+\"\"\"Inspect native Codex skills registry over stdio without a model turn.\"\"\"\n+import json\n+from pathlib import Path\n+import queue\n+import shutil\n+import subprocess\n+import threading\n+import time\n+\n+OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent\n+ROOT = OUT.parents[1]\n+launcher = Path.home() / 'AppData/Roaming/npm/node_modules/@openai/codex/bin/codex.js'\n+cmd = [shutil.which('node'), str(launcher), 'app-server', '--stdio']\n+events = queue.Queue()\n+stderr_path = OUT / 'codex-registry.stderr.txt'\n+with stderr_path.open('w', encoding='utf-8') as stderr:\n+    proc = subprocess.Popen(cmd, cwd=ROOT, stdin=subprocess.PIPE, stdout=subprocess.PIPE,\n+                            stderr=stderr, text=True, encoding='utf-8', errors='replace')\n+    def collect():\n+        for line in proc.stdout:\n+            events.put(line)\n+    threading.Thread(target=collect, daemon=True).start()\n+    transcript = []\n+    def send(value):\n+        proc.stdin.write(json.dumps(value) + '\\n')\n+        proc.stdin.flush()\n+    def response(ident):\n+        deadline = time.monotonic() + 45\n+        while time.monotonic() < deadline:\n+            try:\n+                line = events.get(timeout=min(1, max(.01, deadline-time.monotonic())))\n+            except queue.Empty:\n+                if proc.poll() is not None:\n+                    raise RuntimeError(f'App server exited {proc.returncode}')\n+                continue\n+            transcript.append(line)\n+            value = json.loads(line)\n+            if value.get('id') == ident:\n+                return value\n+        raise TimeoutError(f'No response for {ident}')\n+    try:\n+        send({'id': 1, 'method': 'initialize', 'params': {'clientInfo': {\n+            'name': 'skills_audit_registry', 'version': '1.0'},\n+            'capabilities': {'experimentalApi': True}}})\n+        initialized = response(1)\n+        if 'error' in initialized:\n+            raise RuntimeError(str(initialized['error']))\n+        send({'method': 'initialized', 'params': {}})\n+        send({'id': 2, 'method': 'skills/list', 'params': {\n+            'cwds': [str(ROOT)], 'forceReload': True}})\n+        registry = response(2)\n+        (OUT / 'codex-registry.response.json').write_text(json.dumps(registry, indent=2), encoding='utf-8')\n+        groups = registry.get('result', {}).get('data', [])\n+        summary = {'method': 'skills/list', 'model_turn': False,\n+                   'cwd': str(ROOT), 'protocol_error': registry.get('error'),\n+                   'entries': [{'cwd': entry['cwd'], 'count': len(entry['skills']),\n+                                'errors': entry['errors'],\n+                                'repo_skills': [{'name': s['name'], 'path': s['path'], 'enabled': s['enabled']}\n+                                                for s in entry['skills'] if s['scope'] == 'repo']}\n+                               for entry in groups]}\n+        (OUT / 'codex-registry.receipt.json').write_text(json.dumps(summary, indent=2), encoding='utf-8')\n+        print(json.dumps(summary))\n+    finally:\n+        (OUT / 'codex-registry.jsonl').write_text(''.join(transcript), encoding='utf-8')\n+        proc.terminate()\n+        proc.wait(timeout=10)\n*** End Patch"));
text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:"python audits/skills-2026-10-11-native/probe-codex-registry.py","yield_time_ms":1000,"max_output_tokens":1500}));

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 6.0 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"e7180e","wall_time_seconds":0.5085192,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":618,"output":"\\"\\"\\"Fresh native CLI probes with no write, send or paid API tools enabled.\\"\\"\\"\\r\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\r\\nimport argparse\\r\\nimport json\\r\\nimport shutil\\r\\nimport subprocess\\r\\nimport time\\r\\n\\r\\nROOT=Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]\\r\\nOUT=Path(__file__).resolve().parent\\r\\nparser=argparse.ArgumentParser()\\r\\nparser.add_argument(\'runtime\',choices=[\'codex\',\'claude\'])\\r\\nparser.add_argument(\'case\')\\r\\nparser.add_argument(\'prompt_file\')\\r\\nargs=parser.parse_args()\\r\\nprompt=Path(args.prompt_file).read_text(encoding=\'utf-8\')\\r\\nif args.runtime==\'codex\':\\r\\n    # Use the node launcher directly; PowerShell shims cannot be subprocess executables.\\r\\n    launcher=Path.home()/\'AppData/Roaming/npm/node_modules/@openai/codex/bin/codex.js\'\\r\\n    # Match this chat\'s authorized runtime profile. Windows read-only helper\\r\\n    # refresh conflicts with the desktop\'s active node_repl executable lock.\\r\\n    # The probe prompt itself limits this run to read-only inspection.\\r\\n    cmd=[shutil.which(\'node\'),str(launcher),\'exec\',\'--json\',\'--color\',\'never\',\'-s\',\'danger-full-access\',\'-C\',str(ROOT),\'-\']\\r\\nelse:\\r\\n    cmd=[shutil.which(\'claude\'),\'--print\',\'--output-format\',\'stream-json\',\'--verbose\',\\r\\n         \'--permission-mode\',\'dontAsk\',\'--tools\',\'Read,Glob,Grep,Skill\',\\r\\n         \'--allowedTools\',\'Read,Glob,Grep,Skill\']\\r\\nstart=time.time()\\r\\nresult=subprocess.run(cmd,input=prompt,cwd=ROOT,\\r\\n                      capture_output=True,text=True,encoding=\'utf-8\',errors=\'replace\',timeout=300)\\r\\nprefix=OUT/(args.runtime+\'-\'+args.case)\\r\\nprefix.with_suffix(\'.jsonl\').write_text(result.stdout,encoding=\'utf-8\')\\r\\nprefix.with_suffix(\'.stderr.txt\').write_text(result.stderr,encoding=\'utf-8\')\\r\\nreceipt=dict(runtime=args.runtime,case=args.case,exit_code=result.returncode,seconds=round(time.time()-start,2),\\r\\n             stdout_file=str(prefix.with_suffix(\'.jsonl\')),stderr_file=str(prefix.with_suffix(\'.stderr.txt\')))\\r\\nprefix.with_suffix(\'.receipt.json\').write_text(json.dumps(receipt,indent=2),encoding=\'utf-8\')\\r\\nprint(json.dumps(receipt))\\r\\nfor line in result.stdout.splitlines():\\r\\n    try:\\r\\n        event=json.loads(line)\\r\\n    except ValueError:\\r\\n        continue\\r\\n    if event.get(\'type\') in (\'result\',\'error\'):\\r\\n        print(json.dumps({k:v for k,v in event.items() if k in (\'type\',\'subtype\',\'result\',\'is_error\',\'errors\')}))\\r\\n    if event.get(\'type\')==\'item.completed\' and event.get(\'item\',{}).get(\'type\')==\'agent_message\':\\r\\n        print(event[\'item\'].get(\'text\',\'\')[:5000])\\r\\nraise SystemExit(result.returncode)\\r\\n"}'}]

Also use for model choice/migration, prompting, SDKs, Responses, Realtime, agents, evals, and Chat/Work/Codex comparisons. Do not use for generic app/software tasks that merely mention Codex.\\"\\r\\nmetadata:\\r\\n  short-description: \\"Codex models/pricing, scheduled tasks, skills, settings, setup, troubleshooting, and self-knowledge; OpenAI APIs and ChatGPT Work. \'You\'/\'this app\' means Codex only.\\"\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# OpenAI Docs\\r\\n\\r\\nProvide current, cited OpenAI product, API, model, and Codex guidance. Read zero or one primary reference.\\r\\n\\r\\n**First substantive action:** Search the user\'s exact requested official OpenAI documentation topic and any explicitly named model using a concise, topic-specific query of 2-6 essential terms. When an already-available direct official documentation search and page-retrieval capability is present, use it first: search, then fetch or open the matching official page before general web search. Otherwise, immediately use official-domain web search, then actually open or fetch the relevant official page. Complete this source order before reading a reference, inspecting local or repository files, running a Codex manual or model resolver, drafting a plan, or answering from memory. Use the actual fetched page, not a search snippet or an unopened link. If one official search or page does not establish the answer, search another appropriate official domain and actually open or fetch the result. Preserve the exact requested model; never substitute a newer model.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Only exception:** An explicitly requested, genuinely broad, cross-topic Codex setup, orientation, or system-map synthesis may use the manual first when shell execution and an allowed temporary cache are available. A specific Codex feature, setting, command, error, model, or requested citation remains docs-first. Mixed Chat/Work/Codex comparisons are official documentation questions, not manual-first Codex requests.\\r\\n\\r\\nFor generic software tasks, answer the software task directly. OpenAI implementation, debugging, SDK, API, prompting, agent, and eval requests are not generic.\\r\\n\\r\\nFor a straightforward factual or citation-only request, follow the source order and do not read a route reference. This includes straightforward API facts, ChatGPT Work or mixed Chat/Work/Codex comparisons, model tiers, aliases, Pro mode, reasoning settings, factual migration baselines, and narrow Codex facts. Prioritize `learn.chatgpt.com` for ChatGPT Work.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Choose one primary route\\r\\n\\r\\nUse the first matching route, and read its reference only when the requested task needs that specialized workflow:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Explicitly requested local documentation integration:** Read [integration guidance](references/mcp-diagnostics.md) only when the user explicitly requests that local integration.\\r\\n- **Model migration, upgrades, or model-specific prompting:** Read [model-migration.md](references/model-migration.md) for actual migration planning, implementation, dynamic target resolution, or prompt changes. Preserve an explicitly requested target.\\r\\n- **Model selection and comparisons:** Read [model-selection.md](references/model-selection.md) only when nuanced current, latest, default, cost, latency, quality, or modality tradeoffs need more guidance. Do not run a migration resolver for selection alone.\\r\\n- **Product, API, ChatGPT Work, and mixed Chat/Work/Codex documentation:** Read [official-docs.md](references/official-docs.md) only when fetched official pages leave source selection, API schemas, or the requested implementation unresolved. This route is not manual-first.\\r\\n- **Explicitly broad Codex setup, orientation, or cross-topic synthesis:** Read [codex-self-knowledge.md](references/codex-self-knowledge.md) when the eligible Codex manual or deeper Codex procedures are needed.\\r\\n\\r\\nRead at most one primary reference. Do not open every route, bundled model guide, or helper script. Read a supporting reference or run a helper only when the chosen workflow demonstrably needs it.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Source and execution boundaries\\r\\n\\r\\n- Search, open, fetch, and cite only `developers.openai.com`, `platform.openai.com`, and `learn.chatgpt.com`. Cite the page that supports the claim. State uncertainty when official sources do not establish pricing, availability, account access, limits, or behavior.\\r\\n- Preserve an explicitly requested model for selection, migration, and prompting. Resolve an unspecified latest or current migration target only after searching and fetching current official guidance.\\r\\n- Use `references/latest-model.md` only as a disclosed fallback after current official model guidance does not answer the question. Read `references/upgrading-to-gpt-6-astra.md` only for an actual, requested GPT-6 migration; read `references/prompting-guide.md` only for requested prompting work.\\r\\n- Before building, running, editing, debugging, or testing an API-backed app or tool, use `openai-platform-api-key` first when available. Documentation, conceptual examples, model selection, and read-only guidance do not require an API key.\\r\\n- Say \\"OpenAI Docs\\" or \\"official OpenAI documentation\\" in user-facing answers. Keep exact official citations and examples concise.\\r\\n"}}'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 7.3 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"b2112b","wall_time_seconds":0.7696292,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":81,"output":"C:/Users/Joshu/AppData/Roaming/npm/node_modules/@openai/codex\\\\README.md\\nC:/Users/Joshu/AppData/Roaming/npm/node_modules/@openai/codex\\\\node_modules\\\\@openai\\\\codex-win32-x64\\\\README.md\\nC:/Users/Joshu/AppData/Roaming/npm/node_modules/@openai/codex\\\\node_modules\\\\@openai\\\\codex-win32-x64\\\\vendor\\\\x86_64-pc-windows-msvc\\\\bin\\\\codex.exe\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Rethinking skills and prompts for GPT-6 Astra | OpenAI Developers (https://developers.openai.com/blog/rethinking-skills-and-prompts-for-gpt-6-astra)\n\ue200cite\ue202turn79search0\ue201 [wordlim: 200] Published: last month; Crawled: today; People now default to packaging a lot of skills into their projects, and each skill comes with a name and description that are loaded into the model’s context so it knows when to use them.But many descriptions are far too long, and when you add too many skills, Codex starts shortening their descriptions to fit.\n\nSep 11, 2026 Codex\n\n# Rethinking skills and prompts for GPT-6 Astra\n\nRevisit skill descriptions, AGENTS.md, and task prompts to avoid bloated context.\n\nAuthor: Eric Provencher\n\nCoding agents have come a long way, and best practices are changing fast. With more capable models, what used to require a lot of handholding and scaffolding no longer does.\n\nIf you’ve been using agents like Codex for your projects over the last year, you’ve likely accumulated a lot of instructions as you worked to steer the models toward good outcomes. With each release, it’s been worth revisiting those assumptions, but with GPT-6 Astra, it’s more important than ever.\n\nThese instructions can take many forms: skills, `AGENTS.md`, and your task prompts are all shaping how the model gets work done.\n\n## Better skills\n\nThese instructions can be in the form of skills, which are essentially prompts stored as Markdown files that can also be packaged with resources and bundled scripts. Generally, they are most useful for guidance around a specific workflow, or when using certain apps.\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nSkills | OpenAI API (https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/tools-skills)\n\ue200cite\ue202turn79search1\ue201 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: today; The harness uses them to discover skills; this setup doesn’t use the hosted shell’s `skill_reference` attachment format. ... Use this environment configuration in the session-creation request: ... Once the sandbox becomes available, the harness searches these directories for `SKILL.md` files and adds each discovered skill’s name and description to context. ... For Responses API shell tools, the platform adds each available skill’s `name`, `description`, and `path` to user prompt context so the model knows the skill exists.\n    2\n    3\n    4\n    5\n    6---\n    name: basic-math\n    description: Add or multiply numbers.\n    ---\n    \n    Use this skill when you need a quick sum or product of numbers.`\n\nDuring skill discovery, the model sees the skill’s name and description. Write a description that explains both what the skill does and when to use it. For example, “Review and redline vendor agreements using the fallback clauses” gives the model more useful context than “Helps with legal work.”\n\nKeep the main instructions in `SKILL.md` and link to supporting files as needed:\n    \n    `review-pr/\n    ├── SKILL.md\n    ├── references/\n    │   └── review-guidelines.md\n    ├── scripts/\n    │   └── check-changes.sh\n    └── assets/\n        └── review-template.md`\n\nUse `references/` for background material, `scripts/` for repeatable actions, and `assets/` for reusable templates.\n\n## Create a skill\n\nYou can upload a directory as multipart form data or upload a `.zip` that contains a single top-level folder.\n\n### Option 1: Directory upload (multipart)\n\nUpload multiple `files[]` parts. Each part includes the path inside a single top-level folder.\n\nCreate a skill (multipart)\n    \n    `1\n    2\n    3\n    4 curl -X POST \'https://api.openai.com/v1/skills\' \\\n      -H "Authorization: [REDACTED] $OPENAI_API_KEY" \\\n      -F \'files[]=@./basic_math/SKILL.md;filename=basic_math/SKILL.md;type=text/markdown\' \\\n      -F \'files[]=@./basic_math/calculate.py;filename=basic_math/calculate.py;type=text/plain\'`\n\n### Option 2: Zip upload\n\nZip the top-level folder and upload the zip file.\n\nCreate a skill (zip)\n    \n    `1\n    2\n    3 curl -X POST \'https://api.openai.com/v1/skills\' \\\n      -H "Authorization: [REDACTED] $OPENAI_API_KEY" \\\n      -F \'files=@./basic_math.zip;type=application/zip\'`\n\n        "capability_directories": [\n          "/workspace/capabilities/legal",\n          "/workspace/capabilities/engineering"\n        ]\n      }\n    }`\n\nCapability directories have these requirements:\n\n  * Paths must point to directories inside the sandbox.\n  * Paths must be absolute and unique, and cannot contain `.` or `..` path segments.\n  * A session can register up to 32 capability directories.\n  * Directories must already exist in the environment.\n\nOnce the sandbox becomes available, the harness searches these directories for `SKILL.md` files and adds each discovered skill’s name and description to context. The model can select relevant skills and read their full instructions and supporting files.\n\nSee Agent configuration for session setup and Connect a sandbox for the execution environment. Review the skills and their supporting files before making them available to the agent, and follow the sandbox security guidance.\n\n## Skills in the user prompt\n\nFor Responses API shell tools, the platform adds each available skill’s `name`, `description`, and `path` to user prompt context so the model knows the skill exists.\n\nThe model decides whether to invoke a skill based on this metadata. If the model invokes a skill, it uses the `path` to read the full Markdown instructions from `SKILL.md`.\n\nSkill instructions are user prompt input (not system prompt input), so they’re handled with the same priority as other user-provided instructions. For explicit control, you can still instruct the model to “use the `<skill name>` skill.”\n\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nGPT-5.3-Codex Model | OpenAI API (https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/models/gpt-5.3-codex)\n\ue200cite\ue202turn79search2\ue201 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: today; GPT-5.3-Codex supports `low`, `medium`, `high`, and `xhigh` reasoning effort settings. ... 400,000 context window128,000 max output tokens ... Reasoning token support\n\nModels\n\nImage: gpt-5.3-codex\n\nGPT-5.3-Codex\n\nDeprecated\n\nDefault\n\nThe most capable agentic coding model to date.\n\nCompare\n\nReasoning\n\nSpeed\n\nPrice\n\n$1.75•$14\n\nInput\n\nOutput\n\nGPT-5.3-Codex is optimized for agentic coding tasks in Codex or similar environments. GPT-5.3-Codex supports `low`, `medium`, `high`, and `xhigh` reasoning effort settings. If you want to learn more about prompting GPT-5.3-Codex, refer to our dedicated guide.\n\n400,000 context window\n\n128,000 max output tokens\n\nAug 31, 2025 knowledge cutoff\n\nReasoning token support\n\nPricing\n\nPricing is based on the number of tokens used, or other metrics based on the model type. Declare the skill directory and MCP configuration in `.codex-plugin/plugin.json`: ...     \t"context"\n\n# Plugins\n\nPackage skills and MCP configuration for use across sessions.\n\nCopy Page\n\nA plugin packages skills, MCP configuration, or both. Load its files into your own environment or upload a ZIP to an OpenAI-hosted environment.\n\n## Package the plugin\n\nThis plugin combines a documentation-search skill with the OpenAI documentation MCP. It needs network access but no credentials or local server dependencies.\n    \n    `docs-helper/\n    ├── .codex-plugin/plugin.json\n    ├── .mcp.json\n    └── skills/docs-search/SKILL.md`\n\nDeclare the skill directory and MCP configuration in `.codex-plugin/plugin.json`:\n    \n    1 2 3 4 5 6 7`{\n      "name": "docs-helper",\n      "version": "1.0.0",\n      "description": "Find answers in OpenAI developer documentation.",\n      "skills": "./skills/",\n      "mcpServers": "./.mcp.json"\n    }`\n\nPaths resolve from the plugin root. They must start with `./`, stay inside the plugin, and contain no `..` components. See Package your plugin for the full manifest format.\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nSkills – Plugins | OpenAI Developers (https://developers.openai.com/plugins/concepts/skills)\n\ue200cite\ue202turn79search6\ue201 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: yesterday; Skills are folders of instructions and resources that teach ChatGPT and Codex how to complete repeatable workflows.\n\n# Skills\n\nUnderstand how skills add repeatable workflows around MCP tools.\n\nSkills are folders of instructions and resources that teach ChatGPT and Codex how to complete repeatable workflows. In an MCP-backed plugin, skills complement the server by teaching the model how to combine its tools for recognizable user goals.\n\nEach skill has a `SKILL.md` file with:\n\n  * A name.\n  * A description that tells the model when to consider the skill.\n  * Instructions for completing the workflow.\n  * Optional references, scripts, templates, and other assets.\n\n## How skills complement an MCP server\n\nAn MCP server provides live information and controlled actions. A skill provides the workflow around those tools: when to call them, in what order, how to handle incomplete results, and what the final output should contain.\n\nFor example, a skill can define how to:\n\n  * Retrieve account activity and turn it into a customer briefing.\n  * Review project data, identify risks, and draft a status update.\n  * Combine search and fetch tools into a sourced research workflow.\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nGPT-5.1-Codex-Max Model | OpenAI API (https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/models/gpt-5.1-codex-max)\n\ue200cite\ue202turn79search7\ue201 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: today; Learn how to get the most of GPT-5.1-Codex-Max in the prompting guide.400,000 context window128,000 max output tokens ... But many descriptions are far too long, and when you add too many skills, Codex starts shortening their descriptions to fit. The model ends up seeing less of each description, making it harder to know which skill to pick.\nL910: What’s worse is that descriptions can often contradict each other or over-emphasize when skills should be used, leading the model to load instructions that don’t actually help the task.\nL911: \nL912: A common workflow to create skills is to use the `$skill-creator` skill. 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.\\\\n\\\\nActive in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:\\\\n\\\\n| Skill | Use it when |\\\\n|---|---|\\\\n| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua\'s explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |\\\\n| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |\\\\n| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |\\\\n| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |\\\\n| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |\\\\n| `transcript-intake` | retrieving, filing or reconciling student/session transcripts; separate from knowledge `ingest` |\\\\n| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |\\\\n| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |\\\\n| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |\\\\n| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |\\\\n| `accommodations-email` | \\\\\\"accommodations email to X\\\\\\" (dictation: \\\\\\"accommodations scale\\\\\\"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |\\\\n\\\\nWhen a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.\\\\n\\\\n## Guardrails\\\\n\\\\n- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.\\\\n- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua\'s explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.\\\\n- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.\\\\n- **Root stays thin.** Don\'t accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.\\\\n- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.\\\\n- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.\\\\n- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don\'t grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.\\\\n- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.\\\\n- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.\\\\n- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.\\\\n- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU\'s recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua\'s explicit approval.\\\\n- **Memory = native automemory.** \\\\\\"Remember that…\\\\\\" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.\\\\n- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.\\\\n\\\\n## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)\\\\n\\\\n- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.\\\\n- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.\\\\n- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.\\\\n- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.\\\\n- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex and Claude both activate the LLM Wiki ingest, query, and lint workflows. Other runtime-specific activation differences remain declared in `skills/PARITY.md`. Never have separate runtimes writing the same page concurrently.\\\\n- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.\\\\n\\\\n</INSTRUCTIONS>\\"\\n"}'}]

## assistant

The supported maximum is 10,000 tokens. The native prompt renderer accepts that override, but the resulting descriptions are still sharply shortened. I’m comparing the rendered catalogs to identify how much coverage the setting actually restores.

text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:"@'\nfrom pathlib import Path\nimport tomllib,hashlib,re,datetime,json\np=Path.home()/'.codex/config.toml'\nb=p.read_bytes(); old=tomllib.loads(b.decode('utf-8-sig'))\nassert old.get('skills',{}).get('max_context_tokens') is None, 'Expected absent skill context setting'\nnewline=b'\\r\\n' if b'\\r\\n' in b else b'\\n'\nassert not re.search(rb'^\\[skills\\]\\s*$',b,re.M), 'Unexpected existing skills header'\nnew=b+(b'' if b.endswith(b'\\n') else newline)+newline+b'[skills]'+newline+b'max_context_tokens = 10000'+newline\nparsed=tomllib.loads(new.decode('utf-8-sig'))\nexpected=dict(old); expected['skills']=dict(old.get('skills',{})); expected['skills']['max_context_tokens']=10000\nassert parsed==expected, 'Unexpected semantic config change'\nbackupdir=p.parent/'backups'; backupdir.mkdir(exist_ok=True)\nbackup=backupdir/('config-skills-context-budget-'+datetime.datetime.now().strftime('%Y%m%d-%H%M%S')+'.toml')\nwith backup.open('xb') as f: f.write(b)\nassert p.read_bytes()==b, 'CAS failed: config changed during read'\np.write_bytes(new)\nactual=p.read_bytes(); verified=tomllib.loads(actual.decode('utf-8-sig'))\nassert actual==new and verified==expected, 'Readback mismatch'\nreceipt={'setting':'skills.max_context_tokens','value':10000,'config_path':str(p),'backup_path':str(backup),'backup_sha256':hashlib.sha256(b).hexdigest(),'config_sha256':hashlib.sha256(actual).hexdigest(),'unrelated_semantic_fields_preserved':True,'original_bytes_preserved_as_prefix':actual.startswith(b),'readback_toml_verified':True}\nPath('audits/skills-2026-10-11-native/codex-skills-context-budget-receipt.json').write_text(json.dumps(receipt,indent=2),encoding='utf-8')\nprint(json.dumps(receipt))\n'@ | python -",max_output_tokens:1000}));
